ParlaMint-NO_2019-06-04.ana.ud396e7266	The President-in-Office of the Council, Tone Trøen, said that the presidency often receives requests from the committees to postpone the time of delivery and to postpone the dates of debate. Yes, it happens often. But I can never remember that it has happened without there being a reason for it. It is true that the committees will have to be delayed and have to have more time before they can be set up. It happens that it has to be moved on debate dates – perhaps because a government council has left. It happens. But then there's a reason. There's no reason why this suddenly had to be postponed. In addition, these cases have been finalised. They were on the agenda for tomorrow's debate. In addition, I have to say, it is a good thing, if things like this happen, that it is included in the committee, then a majority of the committee adopts it – you take a vote, majority against minorities, and the majority is right. We had committee meetings on the transport committee today at 4 p.m. 11, and the government parties did not even find it worth putting it on the agenda, not even informing it under any circumstances, before the opposition parties took it up. It smells like this. This is simply very unfortunate. I think Helge Orten and Morten Stordalen let the cats out of the bag. They were afraid that there would be an extensive toll debate in Stortinget. Morten Stordalen was afraid that there would be a debate on the Progress Party. Think about it! They were afraid that we would debate tolls for two days – that we would spend two days discussing 15 billion. kr instead of spending one day and cutting down on speaking time. No, President, this is very unfortunate, and I must say I am most disappointed that the majority of the presidency has let themselves be used in this game. The members of the Presidency are our trusted electors. The members of the Presidency are elected by confidence for the whole of Stortinget, for all the representatives of big business. And the members of the presidency are responsible for following Storting's Rules of Procedure, for having matters properly dealt with, and for all the parties involved feeling comfortable with the way we deal with matters, and the way in which we decide how the Storting should work. President, it is a pity that the majority of the presidency has allowed one party to be used for purely party-tactical purposes in this matter.
ParlaMint-NO_2019-06-04.ana.ud396e7279	The background to the request of the parties which are in the majority of the Committee on Transport and Communications has been highlighted by speakers other than myself. I explained in my first speech that the majority of the presidency supported the justification given when the request was made. The presidency dealt with it this morning early. I need to comment on the implications of breaches of the Rules of Procedure, because this is not a violation of the Rules of Procedure. The agenda shall be published no later than 24 hours before the staging meeting. Paragraph 35 of the Rules of Procedure states: ‘The published weekly programme referred to in the first and second sentences of the first subparagraph and the agenda published may not be waived without having to do so.’;» The agenda was not published before this resolution was taken, so there is no breach of the Rules of Procedure that has happened here. A decision was taken before noon. 10 today, and then the agenda was announced. It is very important for me to stress, because I believe it was insinuated that there was a breach of the Rules of Procedure, that we waived the Rules of Procedure – that is, it was not done in this case. That happens to be a minority and a majority in the presidency as well. It's rare, but it happens. It has also happened in similar cases in the past. However, I believe that the majority of the Committee has explained – i.e. the parties that represent the majority of the Committee – why they justified the request they made yesterday. So I have probably expressed myself a little inaccuracies in the interview with NRK today. I would say that it was the majority of the parties in the committee and not the majority in the committee. I'm sorry. Then I also notice that in this debate the committee chairman has also mentioned, at least others have mentioned, that he has regretted it at the committee meeting, and that it was an incorrect mention.
